class="mailing-list-message__author">Effenberg, Simon</span><time datetime="2014-07-29T01:36:15-07:00">July 29, 2014 at 1:36 AM</time></header><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Hi,

we have some issues with 2i queries like that:

seffenberg@kriak46-1:~$ while :; do curl -s
localhost:8098/buckets/conversation/index/createdat_int/0/23182680 | ruby
-rjson -e &quot;o = JSON.parse(STDIN.read); puts o[&#x27;keys&#x27;].size&quot;; sleep 1; done

13853
13853
0
557
557
557
13853
0

...

So I tried to start a repair-2i first on one vnode/partition on one node
(which is quiet new in the cluster.. 2 weeks or so).

The command is failing with the following log entries:

seffenberg@kriak46-7:~$ sudo riak-admin repair-2i
22835963083295358096932575511191922182123945984
Will repair 2i on these partitions:
        22835963083295358096932575511191922182123945984
Watch the logs for 2i repair progress reports
seffenberg@kriak46-7:~$ 2014-07-29 08:20:22.729 UTC [info]
&lt;0.5929.1061&gt;@riak_kv_2i_aae:init:139 Starting 2i repair at speed 100 for
partitions [22835963083295358096932575511191922182123945984]
2014-07-29 08:20:22.729 UTC [info]
&lt;0.5930.1061&gt;@riak_kv_2i_aae:repair_partition:257 Acquired lock on partition
22835963083295358096932575511191922182123945984
2014-07-29 08:20:22.729 UTC [info]
&lt;0.5930.1061&gt;@riak_kv_2i_aae:repair_partition:259 Repairing indexes in
partition 22835963083295358096932575511191922182123945984
2014-07-29 08:20:22.740 UTC [info]
&lt;0.5930.1061&gt;@riak_kv_2i_aae:create_index_data_db:324 Creating temporary
database of 2i data in /var/lib/riak/anti_entropy/2i/tmp_db
2014-07-29 08:20:22.751 UTC [info]
&lt;0.5930.1061&gt;@riak_kv_2i_aae:create_index_data_db:361 Grabbing all index data
for partition 22835963083295358096932575511191922182123945984
2014-07-29 08:25:22.752 UTC [info]
&lt;0.5929.1061&gt;@riak_kv_2i_aae:next_partition:160 Finished 2i repair:
        Total partitions: 1
        Finished partitions: 1
        Speed: 100
        Total 2i items scanned: 0
        Total tree objects: 0
        Total objects fixed: 0
With errors:
Partition: 22835963083295358096932575511191922182123945984
Error: index_scan_timeout

2014-07-29 08:25:22.752 UTC [error] &lt;0.4711.1061&gt; gen_server &lt;0.4711.1061&gt;
terminated with reason: bad argument in call to
eleveldb:async_write(#Ref&lt;0.0.10120.211816&gt;, &lt;&lt;&gt;&gt;,
[{put,&lt;&lt;131,104,2,109,0,0,0,20,99,111,110,118,101,114,115,97,116,105,111,110,95,115,101,99,114,...&gt;&gt;,...}],
 []) in eleveldb:write/3 line 155

&lt;0.4711.1061&gt; with 0 neighbours exited with reason: bad argument in call to

[{put,&lt;&lt;131,104,2,109,0,0,0,20,99,111,110,118,101,114,115,97,116,105,111,110,95,115,101,99,114,...&gt;&gt;,...}],
 []) in eleveldb:write/3 line 155 in gen_server:terminate/6 line 747
2014-07-29 08:25:22.753 UTC [error] &lt;0.1031.0&gt; Supervisor
{&lt;0.1031.0&gt;,poolboy_sup} had child riak_core_vnode_worker started with
{riak_core_vnode_worker,start_link,undefined} at &lt;0.4711.1061&gt; exit with reason
bad argument in call to eleveldb:async_write(#Ref&lt;0.0.10120.211816&gt;, &lt;&lt;&gt;&gt;,
[{put,&lt;&lt;131,104,2,109,0,0,0,20,99,111,110,118,101,114,115,97,116,105,111,110,95,115,101,99,114,...&gt;&gt;,...}],
 []) in eleveldb:write/3 line 155 in context child_terminated

Anything I can do about that? What&#x27;s the issue here?

I&#x27;m using Riak 1.4.8 (.deb package).

Cheers
Simon

</div></div></details></li><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg14572" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">Guido Medina</span><time datetime="2014-07-29T01:44:56-07:00">July 29, 2014 at 1:44 AM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: repair-2i stops with &quot;bad argument in call to	eleveldb:async_write&quot;</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Hi Simon,

There are some (maybe related) Level DB fixes in 1.4.9 and 1.4.10, I
don&#x27;t think there isn&#x27;t any harm for you to do a rolling upgrade since
nothing major changed, just bug fixes, here is the release notes&#x27; link
for reference:

<a href="https://github.com/basho/riak/blob/1.4/RELEASE-NOTES.md">https://github.com/basho/riak/blob/1.4/RELEASE-NOTES.md</a>

Best regards,

Guido.
</div><ol class="mailing-list-replies"><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg14573" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">Effenberg, Simon</span><time datetime="2014-07-29T01:48:12-07:00">July 29, 2014 at 1:48 AM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: repair-2i stops with &quot;bad argument in call to	eleveldb:async_write&quot;</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Already started to prepare everything for it.. :)

On Tue, Jul 29, 2014 at 09:43:22AM +0100, Guido Medina wrote:
<blockquote>Hi Simon,

There are some (maybe related) Level DB fixes in 1.4.9 and 1.4.10, I don&#x27;t
think there isn&#x27;t any harm for you to do a rolling upgrade since nothing
major changed, just bug fixes, here is the release notes&#x27; link for
reference:

<a href="https://github.com/basho/riak/blob/1.4/RELEASE-NOTES.md">https://github.com/basho/riak/blob/1.4/RELEASE-NOTES.md</a>

Best regards,

Guido.

reference:

Guido.

</div></div></details></li><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg14612" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">Damian Murphy</span><time datetime="2014-08-04T22:30:07-07:00">August 4, 2014 at 10:30 PM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: repair-2i stops with &quot;bad argument in call	to	eleveldb:async_write&quot;</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Hi Russell,

Just to add a vote that this is not just Simon’s issue - we’re seeing exactly
the same thing that Simon is describing in our Riak 1.4.8. It doesn’t seem to
matter which partition is chosen, nor which of our 5 nodes we run repair-2i on.

The one additional point I’ll make is that this seems to be a built-in 5 minute
timeout - as the error messages I see appear exactly 5 minutes after the
repair-2i operation is initiated no matter how or when I run the process.

Also more than happy to provide logs, etc. if you think it will help.

Cheers,
Damian.

On Tue, Aug 05, 2014 at 10:21:57AM -0400, Engel Sanchez wrote:
<blockquote>Simon:  The data scan for that partition seems to be taking more than 5
minutes to collect a batch of 1000 items, so the 2i repair process is
giving up on it before it has a chance to finish.   You can reduce the
likelihood of this happening by configuring the batch parameter to
something small.  In the riak_kv section of the configuration file, set
this:
{riak_kv, [
{aae_2i_batch_size, 10},
...
Let us know if that allows it to finish the repair.  You should still look
into what may be causing the slowness.  A combination of slow disks or
very large data sets might do it.

</div></div></details></li><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg14614" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">bryan hunt</span><time datetime="2014-08-06T05:10:07-07:00">August 6, 2014 at 5:10 AM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: repair-2i stops with &quot;bad argument in call to	eleveldb:async_write&quot;</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Simon,

If you want to get more verbose logging information, you could perform the
following to change the logging level, to debug, then run `repair-2i`, and
finally switching back to the normal logging level.

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetDebug = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, debug)}
end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetDebug,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from the node

You can then revert back to the normal `info` logging level by running the
following command via `riak attach`:

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetInfo = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, info)} end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetInfo,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from a the node

Please also see the docs for info on `riak attach` monitoring of repairs.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs</a>

Repairs can also be monitored using the `riak-admin transfers` command.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair</a>

Best Regards,

Bryan Hunt

Bryan Hunt - Client Services Engineer - Basho Technologies Limited - Registered
Office - 8 Lincoln’s Inn Fields London WC2A 3BP Reg 07970431
</div></div></details></li><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg14616" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">Engel Sanchez</span><time datetime="2014-08-06T10:01:47-07:00">August 6, 2014 at 10:01 AM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: repair-2i stops with &quot;bad argument in call to	eleveldb:async_write&quot;</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Simon:  The data scan for that partition seems to be taking more than 5
minutes to collect a batch of 1000 items, so the 2i repair process is
giving up on it before it has a chance to finish.   You can reduce the
likelihood of this happening by configuring the batch parameter to
something small.  In the riak_kv section of the configuration file, set
this:

{riak_kv, [
   {aae_2i_batch_size, 10},
   ...

Let us know if that allows it to finish the repair.  You should still look
into what may be causing the slowness.  A combination of slow disks or very
large data sets might do it.

On Wed, Aug 06, 2014 at 01:08:36PM +0100, bryan hunt wrote:
<blockquote>Simon,

If you want to get more verbose logging information, you could perform the
following to change the logging level, to debug, then run `repair-2i`, and
finally switching back to the normal logging level.

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetDebug = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, debug)}
end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetDebug,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from the node

You can then revert back to the normal `info` logging level by running the
following command via `riak attach`:

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetInfo = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, info)}
end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetInfo,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from a the node

Please also see the docs for info on `riak attach` monitoring of repairs.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs</a>

Repairs can also be monitored using the `riak-admin transfers` command.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair</a>

Best Regards,

Bryan Hunt

Bryan Hunt - Client Services Engineer - Basho Technologies Limited -
Registered Office - 8 Lincoln’s Inn Fields London WC2A 3BP Reg 07970431

On Tue, Aug 05, 2014 at 10:21:57AM -0400, Engel Sanchez wrote:
<blockquote>Simon:  The data scan for that partition seems to be taking more than 5
minutes to collect a batch of 1000 items, so the 2i repair process is
giving up on it before it has a chance to finish.   You can reduce the
likelihood of this happening by configuring the batch parameter to
something small.  In the riak_kv section of the configuration file, set
this:
{riak_kv, [
{aae_2i_batch_size, 10},
...
Let us know if that allows it to finish the repair.  You should still
look
into what may be causing the slowness.  A combination of slow disks or
very large data sets might do it.

On Wed, Aug 06, 2014 at 01:08:36PM +0100, bryan hunt wrote:
<blockquote>Simon,

If you want to get more verbose logging information, you could perform the
following to change the logging level, to debug, then run `repair-2i`, and
finally switching back to the normal logging level.

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetDebug = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, debug)}
end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetDebug,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from the node

You can then revert back to the normal `info` logging level by running the
following command via `riak attach`:

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetInfo = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, info)}
end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetInfo,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from a the node

Please also see the docs for info on `riak attach` monitoring of repairs.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs</a>

Repairs can also be monitored using the `riak-admin transfers` command.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair</a>

Best Regards,

Bryan Hunt

Bryan Hunt - Client Services Engineer - Basho Technologies Limited -

On Tue, Aug 05, 2014 at 10:21:57AM -0400, Engel Sanchez wrote:
<blockquote>Simon:  The data scan for that partition seems to be taking more than 5
minutes to collect a batch of 1000 items, so the 2i repair process is
giving up on it before it has a chance to finish.   You can reduce the
likelihood of this happening by configuring the batch parameter to
something small.  In the riak_kv section of the configuration file, set
this:
{riak_kv, [
{aae_2i_batch_size, 10},
...
Let us know if that allows it to finish the repair.  You should still
look
into what may be causing the slowness.  A combination of slow disks or
very large data sets might do it.

</div></div></details></li><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg14625" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">Effenberg, Simon</span><time datetime="2014-08-08T01:14:25-07:00">August 8, 2014 at 1:14 AM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: repair-2i stops with &quot;bad argument in call to	eleveldb:async_write&quot;</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Hi Bryan,

On Wed, Aug 06, 2014 at 01:08:36PM +0100, bryan hunt wrote:
<blockquote>Simon,

If you want to get more verbose logging information, you could perform the
following to change the logging level, to debug, then run `repair-2i`, and
finally switching back to the normal logging level.

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetDebug = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, debug)}
end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetDebug,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from the node

You can then revert back to the normal `info` logging level by running the
following command via `riak attach`:

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetInfo = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, info)}
end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetInfo,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from a the node

Please also see the docs for info on `riak attach` monitoring of repairs.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs</a>

Repairs can also be monitored using the `riak-admin transfers` command.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair</a>

Best Regards,

Bryan Hunt

Bryan Hunt - Client Services Engineer - Basho Technologies Limited -
Registered Office - 8 Lincoln’s Inn Fields London WC2A 3BP Reg 07970431

any updates on this issue? I&#x27;m still able to search a range of 2i and

I cannot rely on 2i right now nor can I repair it.

Cheers
Simon

On Wed, Aug 06, 2014 at 01:08:36PM +0100, bryan hunt wrote:
<blockquote>Simon,

If you want to get more verbose logging information, you could perform the
following to change the logging level, to debug, then run `repair-2i`, and
finally switching back to the normal logging level.

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetDebug = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, debug)}
end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetDebug,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from the node

You can then revert back to the normal `info` logging level by running the
following command via `riak attach`:

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetInfo = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;, info)}
end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetInfo,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from a the node

Please also see the docs for info on `riak attach` monitoring of repairs.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs</a>

Repairs can also be monitored using the `riak-admin transfers` command.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair</a>

Best Regards,

Bryan Hunt

Bryan Hunt - Client Services Engineer - Basho Technologies Limited -
Registered Office - 8 Lincoln’s Inn Fields London WC2A 3BP Reg 07970431

On Wed, Aug 06, 2014 at 01:08:36PM +0100, bryan hunt wrote:
<blockquote>Simon,

If you want to get more verbose logging information, you could perform
the following to change the logging level, to debug, then run
`repair-2i`, and finally switching back to the normal logging level.

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetDebug = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;,
debug)} end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetDebug,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from the node

You can then revert back to the normal `info` logging level by running
the following command via `riak attach`:

- `riak attach`
- `(riak@nodename)1&gt; SetInfo = fun() -&gt; {node(),
lager:set_loglevel(lager_file_backend, &quot;/var/log/riak/console.log&quot;,
info)} end.`
- `(riak@nodename)2&gt; rp(rpc:multicall(erlang, apply, [SetInfo,[]])).`
(don&#x27;t forget the period at the end of these statements)
- Hit CTRL+C twice to quit from a the node

Please also see the docs for info on `riak attach` monitoring of repairs.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Monitoring-Repairs</a>

Repairs can also be monitored using the `riak-admin transfers` command.

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair">http://docs.basho.com/riak/1.4.9/ops/running/recovery/repairing-partitions/#Running-a-Repair</a>

Best Regards,

Bryan Hunt

Bryan Hunt - Client Services Engineer - Basho Technologies Limited -
Registered Office - 8 Lincoln’s Inn Fields London WC2A 3BP Reg 07970431

</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>...
